Real Estate Is Attractively Valued, But Flows Have Been A Problem
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy, Bloomberg, S&P Capital IQ / Clarifi
▪
Real Estate has been the 3rd best performing sector in the S&P 500 since the Tech trade peaked in early October, after a tough summer. 
▪
Continues to screen attractive from a valuation perspective, with the sector’s median price-to-FFO (P/FFO) ratio sitting below long-term 
averages (on a weighted basis, valuations are slightly below the historical average). 
▪
Flows have been weak to the sector, but do show signs of improvement in recent weeks. 
▪
In our September analyst survey, the sector registered a mixed scorecard, with neutral outlook for performance over the next 6-12 months 
and the domestic policy backdrop, alongside strong, negative assessment of valuations. Analysts, however, had a constructive view on 
demand. 
▪
We continue to monitor interest rate trends (with concerns about impact from a prolonged higher interest rate environment) and housing 
affordability (the latter was a topic in the midterm campaigns in some races). Relative performance tends to move inversely with interest 
rates and in sync with consumer confidence, meaning it should outperform if confidence rises and rates fall, but underperform if they do 
the opposite.

RBC Capital Markets

Utilities Challenged by Pricey P/Es & AI Angst, Though Its Defensive Nature Has Won Out of Late
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy, Bloomberg, S&P Capital IQ / Clarifi
▪
After a difficult summer, has outperformed since the Tech trade peaked since early October. Still, it’s middle of the pack vs. other sectors. 
▪
The sector continues to have expensive valuations despite its late summer and early fall underperformance. Admittedly, valuations have 
been trading in a higher range over the past decade, but we’d like to see them get closer to neutral before jumping back in. 
▪
EPS revisions are both positive and improving in recent updates.
▪
Flows had improved in recent weeks, but in a recent update registered the largest weekly outflow since 2022. 
▪
In our September analyst survey, Utilities had a mixed outlook with neutral views on performance and valuations, alongside the strongest 
demand outlook across sectors. Our analyst expressed one of the most negative views on the domestic policy backdrop. On the 
midterms, Utilities stood out with the most negative outlook under a Democratic sweep scenario, highlighting the sector’s sensitivity to 
potential changes in the policy environment. 
▪
Political dynamics and the debate around affordability still seem like a headwind for this sector given its link to the AI trade and the 
outcome of the midterm elections.

RBC Capital Markets
147
RBC Analyst Survey – US Heat Map
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy, RBC Capital Markets 
▪
When we look specifically at our US analysts’ views, several things jump out:
▪
Overall views: Our US analysts have a constructive view on valuations and performance and demand. They are less constructive on the 
domestic policy backdrop and veered closer to mixed/neutral. 
▪
Performance outlooks by US sector: Most sectors had a constructive tilt on performance over the next 6-12 months with the most 
constructive views on Materials, Tech, Energy and Financials. The exceptions were Utilities and Real Estate, which were neutral, and 
Consumer Staples, Consumer Discretionary, and Communication Services, which each came in negative. Interestingly, the view on 
Consumer Discretionary deteriorated further since the previous survey. 
▪
Valuation views by US sector: Valuation assessments were more mixed than the overall US score on this question indicates. Positive 
valuation views are seen for Financials, Communication Services and Materials. Valuation came in negative for Real Estate and came in 
neutral/mixed for other sectors. 
▪
Demand views by US sector: Views on demand are mixed across sectors. Demand is seen as the most constructive for Utilities, followed 
by Real Estate, Tech and Consumer Staples. Consumer Discretionary and Materials have clear negative tilts. 
▪
Domestic policy backdrop by US sector: Views varied by sector. There are positive tilts for Financials and Energy and negative tilts for 
Consumer Staples and Utilities. Other sectors came in neutral/mixed on this question. 
Key Takeaways

Comm Services, Cons Discretionary & Financials Stocks Most Inversely Correlated With Gas & Diesel 
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy, Bloomberg, S&P Capital IQ / Clarifi
▪
Much uncertainty in the energy price outlook remains. Using qual weighted versions of the sector and broader market indices, our 
analysis suggests that stocks in the S&P 500 Comm Services, Consumer Discretionary, and Financials sectors should outperform if gas 
and diesel prices fall. They could also be expected to underperform if they fall. Energy is the main sector that benefits when energy prices 
go up. 
▪
Note we used equal weighted indices given the heavy weighting of internet names that are considered to be more like Tech in the Comm 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. 
Key Takeaways

RBC Capital Markets
160
S&P 500 Sector Playbook During The Past Five Hiking Cycles
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy, Haver, Bloomberg, S&P Capital IQ/Clarifi, S&P 
▪
Looking from the first to the last hike, what stands out the most to us is that Energy and Technology – an instigator of inflation and a tool 
to combat it – had the best track records in terms of performance relative to the S&P 500. 
▪
Other sectors lagged more often than they led, but the only consistent underperformer in every hiking cycle was Communication Services 
– a sector that has a fair amount of consumer exposure. Note that Staples and Discretionary lagged in 4/5 hiking cycles as well, but it’s 
worth noting that other sectors also tended to underperform more often than they outperformed. 
Key Takeaways

RBC Capital Markets
162
We Are Keeping Eye on The Key Issues That Drove Voters to the Polls
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy
▪
One of the most deeply consensus views in the financial community is that the new Congress will accomplish very little due to Trump’s 
veto power. Note, a veto can be overridden by two thirds of the Senate and House (67 and 290, assuming all members are present with a 
quorum).  Congress could also push back against the war through constraints on spending.   Note, in close Senate races, candidates on 
both sides also pledged to take a bipartisan approach. 
▪
Issue wise, based on what we heard in the campaigns, we would not be surprised to see Democrats try to roll back Medicaid and SNAP 
cuts from last year’s tax bill, work on a ban on Congressional stock trading, seek gas/diesel price relief (several Senate candidates argued 
for a suspension of a Federal gas tax), or pursue middle class tax breaks. We also have a difficult time thinking Congress won’t take up 
something on AI/data center regulation given recent developments in the news. 
Key Takeaways

Summary Of Sector Views On The US Midterm Elections From Our US Analysts
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy, Bloomberg, RBC Capital Markets 
▪
In terms of the impact on the outlook for performance, our US analysts had a constructive tilt in a Republican sweep scenario, a slightly 
negative tilt on a Democratic sweep scenario, and a neutral stance on a Republican Senate/Democratic House scenario. 
▪
In a Democratic sweep scenario, our analysts generally had a negative tilt, particularly on Utilities. The main exceptions were Consumer 
Discretionary (positive), Communication Services (neutral), Consumer Staples (neutral), Real Estate (neutral) and Tech (neutral). 
▪
In the survey, which was taken late September, our analysts were asked to evaluate the impact to their industries from a policy 
perspective on a scale of +/-2 with +2 being very bullish and -2 being very bearish. 0 was neutral or mixed.

RBC Capital Markets
165
Our Current Take On The Drivers of Small vs. Large – Preference For Small Caps
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy
Flows – Better In Large Cap, But Signs of a Shift at the Margin
Overall, we’ve been seeing inflows to Large Cap funds and outflows from Small Cap funds. Things are shifting a bit, however, as Small 
Cap flows have made some forays into positive territory on recent weekly updates. 
Investor Sentiment & Positioning – Small Caps Have Been Deeply Out of Favor, While Mega Cap Growth Has Been Crowded
Deep net shorts for Russell 2000 futures have been in place per CFTC’s data, which are in the process of closing. This stands in contrast 
to more elevated positioning for US equity futures generally and Nasdaq futures, in particular. Small Caps normally don’t outperform when 
the VIX spikes sharply or high yield credit spreads widen. We’ve been surprised to see they’ve held up better than Large Cap amid the 
recently volatility. 
Valuations – Small Caps Look Cheap Relative to Their Own History
Russell 2000 forward P/Es recently felt a little below their Iran war lows but didn’t match their early 2025 lows which were consistent with 
a typical deeply oversold signal. Still, unlike S&P 500 or Nasdaq 100 forward P/Es, the Russell 2000’s forward P/Es have broken below 
their long-term averages. Small looks cheap on a relative P/E vs. Large, but we don’t pay too much attention to this stat anymore as we’ve 
found different size and style indices tend to get different P/Es based on differences in their ROEs (earnings quality). 
Earnings – Small Caps Expected To See Faster Growth Rates In Early 2027, But Revisions Have Been Under More Pressure
Looking at bottom-up consensus estimates, the Russell 2000 is expected to see faster EPS growth than the S&P 500 and its AI focused 
names in early 2027, though this gap with the AI names is expected to close later in the year. R2000 has seen less robust EPS revisions. 
Small
Large
Favors
S&P 500 Outlook / Overview
Economic Backdrop – Economic Tailwinds Favor Small Cap, But Risks Require Close Monitoring
Small Caps tend to outperform when GDP forecasts and ISM manufacturing are rising (two trends which are both underway) and jobs 
growth is accelerating (this indicator had stalled a bit but is generally resilient). Rate hikes could derail this narrative eventually. 
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates – More of A Headwind For Small Caps For Now
Small Caps tend to underperform when more hikes are coming into the consensus forecast, something that has returned recently. 
Government Policy – More Favorable Political Winds For Small Caps
The political winds coming out of the midterms were more favorable to more cyclical and consumer areas given voters’ desire to see 
something done on affordability and get out of the Iran way, and the anti AI/data center sentiment that may drive action at the state and 
local level, creating more friction for companies directly impacted by the AI theme. It is admittedly unlikely Congress will accomplish much.
Deals & Cash Deployment – Small Cap Balance Sheets Are Healthier Than Feared, With M&A a Potential Catalyst
In a rising rate environment, Small Caps are disadvantaged by higher debt burdens, though our work indicates these are in good shape 
relative to history. All size segments could benefit from a renewal of M&A, which may accelerate ahead of the 2028 Presidential election. 
We think this could be an important driver for Small Caps if a wave of corporate buying ensues. 
Mixed

RBC Capital Markets
166
Small Cap Inflections Have Been Tied To Key Events Over The Past Few Years
▪
Though it took them a few months to inflect higher, Small Caps essentially bottomed relative to Large Caps shortly after the Liberation Day tariffs were announced. 
▪
The mid 2026 peak came around the annual Reconstitution as well as when Fed hikes started to get baked into US equity markets. 
▪
The most recent low in mid October came shortly after the big cap Tech trade peaked earlier in the month, highlighting how Small Caps have benefited from the 
derisking of the AI trade that’s been underway. That bottom was also aided by an easing of interest rate fears, which returned in mid November when Waller began 
discussing the possibility of big hikes.  Despite renewed rate fears, Small Caps are holding vs. Large Caps in recent trading. 
Key Takeaways

RBC Capital Markets
172
Russell 2000 Forward P/Es Recently Broke Below Iran War Lows, But Didn’t Match Tariff Lows
▪On an NTM basis, the Small Cap P/e recently broke well below average, but didn’t quite make it back to the Liberation Day lows. Note, this version of 
the P/E has been making higher lows post COVID. 
▪On an FY2 basis, the P/E did recently break a little below its Iran war low, but also failed to match tariff lows. This is important, because Small Cap P/Es 
tend to bottom out in the 11-13x range in recessions and other crises.

Russell 2000
Consensus No Longer Expects Small To Lead S&P 500 AI Names On Net Income Growth In 2027
▪
We had been seeing better net income growth expectations in Small Caps than the S&P 500 AI basket on this data set, but that is no longer present in the latest 
updates. In the latest updates, Russell 2000 is expected to stay ahead of the AI basket through 2Q27, then trend below it for the 2nd half of the year. Russell 2000 is still 
expected to beat the S&P 500 broadly on this metric through the end of 2027, however. 
▪
For 4Q26, Small Caps’ growth rate is 56%, followed by the AI Basket (56%), the S&P 500 (29%), and the S&P 500 Ex-AI Basket (21%). 
▪
For 4Q27, the S&P 500 AI Basket’s growth rate is 38%, followed by Small Caps (29%), the S&P 500 (20%), and the S&P 500 Ex-AI Basket (10%). 
Key Takeaways
Small Caps / Earnings 
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy, Bloomberg
S&P 500 AI Basket includes tickers: ACN, ADBE, AMD, GOOGL, AMZN, ANET, AVGO, DDOG, INTU, META, MU, MSFT, NVDA, ORCL, PLTR, QCOM, NOW, SMCI.

RBC Capital Markets
183
Small Caps Usually Lag When Jobs Growth Is Weak, And Outperform When It Is Strengthening
▪
Generally, neither stalling nor deteriorating jobs growth tends to be good for Small Cap performance relative to Large Caps. Usually, Small Caps need to see 
accelerating jobs growth to outperform. There is a wrinkle to that idea, however. In the last two cycles, Small/Large relative performance has tended to bottom right 
around when deep and sustained jobs losses emerge. 
▪
The strong print for October came in November, helping Smal Cap performance stabilize after the election. Our economists noted the print was solid and points to the 
economy firing on all cylinders.
Key Takeaways
Small Caps / Economic Backdrop

Ramp-Up In IPO Activity Tends To Be Good For Small Caps
▪
Aside from the Tech bubble (late ’90s), rising IPO activity trends to be accompanied by Small Cap outperformance relative to Large Caps. 
▪
We think this is due to the improvement in animal spirits in financial markets and an uptake in risk-taking that generates strength in both.

Within Small Cap, Energy & Tech Also Have The Strongest Revisions Profile
Source: RBC US Equity Strategy, Bloomberg, S&P Capital IQ, CIQ estimates, Russell. Excludes REITs
▪
Similar to Large Cap, Tech and Energy have the strongest profiles in Small Cap.
▪

▪

▪

RBC Capital Markets
211
R3000 Semis FY2 P/E Recently Returned To Levels Well Above Average Before Falling Again
▪
At the recent high, the Semis FY2 P/E had not hit Tech bubble highs but was above GFC and post-GFC highs. It moved lower recently 
and got close to the post-2020 average line. Then it started to become elevated again. In the most recent hit that the group has taken, 
valuations have gotten below the 5-year average and also touched the longer-term average.
▪
Meanwhile, Software valuations recently got close to their GFC lows and appear to be stabilizing. 
▪
The relative P/E of Software relative to Semis got close to Tech bubble and GFC lows and is starting to move up.

RBC Capital Markets
212
Semis EPS Estimate Revisions Slipping, Software EPS Estimate Revisions Also Slipping Lately
▪
One thing we’ve been pointing out in recent client meetings is that Software revisions stabilized around the 50% mark, but didn’t enter 
negative revisions territory. Recently, they’ve been moving up again but are below typical peaks. 
▪
Meanwhile, Semis revisions have started to show more clear signs of deceleration. We are mindful that past peaks for this industry have 
sometimes been extended for multiple years, but do note we’re starting to see a little slippage on this indicator that bears watching 
closely. 
▪
The gap between Software and Semis on this indicator recently retreated to typical lows. In recent updates, the gap is off the recent low 
but Software EPS revisions have worsened more.
Key Takeaways

RBC Capital Markets
213
Within Materials, No Industry Stands Out With Attractive Valuations And Positive EPS Revisions
▪
Valuations for Metals & Mining have fluctuated recently and are currently sitting below their long-term averages, but the industry has 
slightly negative EPS revisions. 
▪
Chemicals, Construction Materials, and Containers & Packaging have expensive valuations and negative EPS revisions.

214
Within Utilities, No Industry Stands Out With Attractive Valuations And Positive EPS Revisions
▪
Independent Power and Renewable Producers industry has negative EPS revisions but attractive valuations.
▪
Electric Utilities, Multi Utilities, and Water Utilities have positive EPS revisions but expensive valuations. 
▪
Gas Utilities has negative EPS revisions and expensive valuations.

The analyst(s) responsible for preparing this research report received compensation that is based upon various factors, including total revenues of the member 
companies of RBC Capital Markets and its affiliates, a portion of which are or have been generated by investment banking activities of the member companies of 
RBC Capital Markets and its affiliates.
With regard to the MAR investment recommendation requirements in relation to relevant securities, a member company of Royal Bank of Canada, together with its 
affiliates, may have a net long or short financial interest in excess of 0.5% of the total issued share capital of the entities mentioned in the investment 
recommendation. Information relating to this is available upon request from your RBC investment advisor or institutional salesperson.
Distribution of Ratings
For the purpose of ratings distributions, regulatory rules require member firms to assign ratings to one of three rating categories - Buy, Hold/Neutral, or Sell - 
regardless of a firm's own rating categories. Although RBC Capital Markets' ratings of Outperform (O), Sector Perform (SP), and Underperform (U) most closely 
correspond to Buy, Hold/Neutral and Sell, respectively, the meanings are not the same because our ratings are determined on a relative basis.
